# Scope and attribution {.unnumbered}

This volume contains sixteen restated problems from the checked core, followed by 493 source-provided statements. It is a partial collection. Source classifications, numbering and transcriptions are not all independently verified. Mixed-domain records may occur in other subject volumes. The corresponding proofs are in the separate worked volume and the individually labeled source-solution files.

The source statements are reproduced under MathNet's CC BY 4.0 fallback where recovered metadata contains no explicit original rights assertion. The complete package preserves original booklet credits and metadata. Figure-dependent statements and records with uncertain or explicit original rights remain source pointers. Original source wording is retained apart from logged typesetting compatibility repairs. The checked core is separately restated and corrected.

MathNet: Shaden Alshammari, Kevin Wen, Abrar Zainal, Mark Hamilton, Navid Safaei, Sultan Albarakati, William T. Freeman and Antonio Torralba, *MathNet: A Global Multimodal Benchmark for Mathematical Reasoning and Retrieval*, ICLR 2026. Sources: https://mathnet.mit.edu/ ; https://huggingface.co/datasets/ShadenA/MathNet ; https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/ . Original source contributors are credited in the ledger and individual problem files.

Read Coverage_Report.md for exact counts, historical gaps, withheld records and the distinction between actual contests, shortlists and selection material. The author credit identifies this compiled edition and its authored expositions, not authorship of the historical problems.

# The sixteen checked-core problems

## G01. Two similarities sharing a secant

**Source:** IMO shortlist 2015, G1. **Record:** `imo_2015_29f541`.

In an acute triangle $ABC$ with orthocenter $H$, complete the parallelogram $ABGH$. On line $GH$ choose $I$ so that $AC$ passes through the midpoint of $HI$. Let $J\ne C$ be the other intersection of $AC$ with $(GCI)$. Prove $IJ=AH$.

## G02. Reflect the two lines

**Source:** IMO shortlist 2015, G2. **Record:** `imo_2015_7c01e3`.

Triangle $ABC$ lies on a circle $\Omega$ with center $O$. A circle centered at $A$ cuts $BC$ at $D,E$ in the order $B,D,E,C$, and cuts the arcs $AB$ and $AC$ of $\Omega$ not containing the third vertex at $F,G$, respectively. Let $K\ne B$ be the intersection of $AB$ with $(BDF)$, and $L\ne C$ the intersection of $AC$ with $(CEG)$. If distinct lines $FK,GL$ meet at $X$, prove $A,X,O$ collinear.

## G03. An angle condition is a distance locus

**Source:** IMO shortlist 2006, G1. **Record:** `imo_2006_e64448`.

Let $I$ be the incenter of $ABC$. An interior point $P$ satisfies $\angle PBA+\angle PCA=\angle PBC+\angle PCB$. Prove $AP\ge AI$, with equality only at $P=I$.

## G04. Reflection completes a cyclicity proof

**Source:** IMO shortlist 2012, G2. **Record:** `imo_2012_758467`.

In cyclic quadrilateral $ABCD$, the diagonals meet at $E$. The extensions of $AD,BC$ beyond $A,B$ meet at $F$. Complete parallelogram $ECGD$, and reflect $E$ in $AD$ to obtain $H$. Prove $D,H,F,G$ concyclic.

## G05. Reflected lines meet on the mirror

**Source:** IMO shortlist 2013, G2. **Record:** `imo_2013_22bd4a`.

Let $M,N$ be the midpoints of $AB,AC$, and $T$ the midpoint of the arc $BC$ not containing $A$ on $(ABC)$. The circle $(AMT)$ meets the perpendicular bisector of $AC$ at an interior point $X$ of $ABC$; similarly $(ANT)$ meets the perpendicular bisector of $AB$ at an interior point $Y$. If $K=MN\cap XY$, prove $KA=KT$.

## G06. Double the cevians

**Source:** IMO shortlist 2014, G1. **Record:** `imo_2014_a0b822`.

In an acute triangle, choose $P,Q$ on $BC$ with $\angle PAB=C$ and $\angle QAC=B$. Extend $AP,AQ$ to $M,N$ so that $PM=PA$ and $QN=QA$. Show that $BM$ and $CN$ meet on $(ABC)$.

## G07. Pack two similar triangles

**Source:** IMO shortlist 2014, G2. **Record:** `imo_2014_32e67b`.

Points $K,L,M$ on $BC,CA,AB$ have concurrent cevians $AK,BL,CM$. Prove that two of $ALM,BMK,CKL$ have inradii whose sum is at least the inradius $r$ of $ABC$.

## G08. Convert intersection ratios into half angles

**Source:** IMO shortlist 2016, G2. **Record:** `imo_2016_022acb`.

Let $I$ be the incenter, $M$ the midpoint of $BC$, and $D$ the foot from $I$ to $BC$. The perpendicular to $AI$ through $I$ meets $AB,AC$ at $F,E$. Let $X\ne A$ be the other common point of $(AEF)$ and $(ABC)$. Prove that $XD$ and $AM$ meet on $(ABC)$.

## G09. The hidden orthocenter of a pentagon

**Source:** IMO shortlist 2017, G1. **Record:** `imo_2017_e0103e`.

A convex pentagon $ABCDE$ satisfies $AB=BC=CD$, $\angle EAB=\angle BCD$, and $\angle EDC=\angle CBA$. Prove that $AC,BD$ and the perpendicular from $E$ to $BC$ concur.

## G10. A half-turn creates the tangent

**Source:** IMO 2017, Problem 4; shortlist G2. **Record:** `imo_2017_bb6ddb`.

Points $R,S$ lie on $\Omega$, and $R'=2S-R$. Let $I$ lie on the smaller arc $RS$. The circle $\Gamma=(ISR')$ meets the tangent to $\Omega$ at $R$ twice; call the nearer intersection to $R$ point $A$. If $AI$ meets $\Omega$ again at $J$, prove $JR'$ tangent to $\Gamma$.

## G11. Two tangents encode a reflection

**Source:** IMO shortlist 2019, G1. **Record:** `imo_2019_be46cd`.

A circle through $A$ meets $AB,AC$ again at $D,E$ and crosses $BC$ at $F,G$ in that order. Tangents at $F$ to $(BDF)$ and at $G$ to $(CEG)$ meet at $T\ne A$. Prove $AT\parallel BC$.

## G12. Close two circles around the target point

**Source:** IMO shortlist 2021, G1. **Record:** `imo_2021_18d688`.

In parallelogram $ABCD$, assume $AC=BC$. Take $P$ beyond $B$ on $AB$. Let $Q\ne D$ be the intersection of $PD$ with $(ACD)$, and $R\ne P$ the intersection of $PC$ with $(APQ)$. Prove $CD,AQ,BR$ concurrent.

## G13. A circle equation linearizes the ratio

**Source:** IMO shortlist 2015, G4. **Record:** `imo_2015_b11e64`.

In an acute triangle $ABC$, let $M$ be the midpoint of $AC$. A circle through $B,M$ meets $BA,BC$ again at $P,Q$. Complete parallelogram $BPTQ$, and assume $T\in(ABC)$. Determine $BT/BM$.

## G14. Transfer a tangent through equal powers

**Source:** IMO shortlist 2015, G6. **Record:** `imo_2015_d499b1`.

An acute triangle $ABC$ has $AB>AC$, orthocenter $H$, base midpoint $M$, and altitude foot $F$ on $BC$. On its circumcircle $\Omega$, choose $Q,K$ so that $\angle AQH=\angle QKH=90^\circ$. Prove that $(KQH)$ and $(KFM)$ are tangent at $K$.

## G15. Equal segments turn spiral similarity into rotation

**Source:** IMO shortlist 2024, G1. **Record:** `imo_2024_a60b6c`.

A cyclic quadrilateral satisfies $AC<BD<AD$ and $\angle DBA<90^\circ$. On the side of $AD$ opposite $C$, choose $E$ with $DE\parallel AB$, $DE=AC$, and $F$ with $AF\parallel CD$, $AF=BD$. Prove that the perpendicular bisectors of $BC,EF$ meet on $(ABCD)$.

## G16. Reflect a vertex in the incenter

**Source:** IMO shortlist 2024, G2. **Record:** `imo_2024_7db46b`.

A triangle has $AB<AC<BC$, incenter $I$, and incircle. The other tangent parallel to $AC$ meets the interior of $BC$ at $X$; the other tangent parallel to $AB$ meets it at $Y$. Let $P\ne A$ be the intersection of $AI$ with $(ABC)$, and let $K,L$ be the midpoints of $AB,AC$. Prove $\angle KIL+\angle YPX=180^\circ$.


# APMO source statements

## 1989 — 3

**Record:** `apm_1989_6cbaa2`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_1989_6cbaa2.json.gz).

Let $A_{1}$, $A_{2}$, $A_{3}$ be three points in the plane, and for convenience, let $A_{4}=A_{1}$, $A_{5}=A_{2}$. For $n=1,2$, and $3$, suppose that $B_{n}$ is the midpoint of $A_{n} A_{n+1}$, and suppose that $C_{n}$ is the midpoint of $A_{n} B_{n}$. Suppose that $A_{n} C_{n+1}$ and $B_{n} A_{n+2}$ meet at $D_{n}$, and that $A_{n} B_{n+1}$ and $C_{n} A_{n+2}$ meet at $E_{n}$. Calculate the ratio of the area of triangle $D_{1} D_{2} D_{3}$ to the area of triangle $E_{1} E_{2} E_{3}$.
Answer: $\frac{25}{49}$.


## 1991 — 1

**Record:** `apm_1991_942510`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_1991_942510.json.gz).

Let $G$ be the centroid of triangle $A B C$ and $M$ be the midpoint of $B C$. Let $X$ be on $A B$ and $Y$ on $A C$ such that the points $X, Y$, and $G$ are collinear and $X Y$ and $B C$ are parallel. Suppose that $X C$ and $G B$ intersect at $Q$ and $Y B$ and $G C$ intersect at $P$. Show that triangle $M P Q$ is similar to triangle $A B C$.


## 1991 — 5

**Record:** `apm_1991_ab7085`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_1991_ab7085.json.gz).

Given are two tangent circles and a point $P$ on their common tangent perpendicular to the lines joining their centres. Construct with ruler and compass all the circles that are tangent to these two circles and pass through the point $P$.


## 1991 — 2

**Record:** `apm_1991_b65647`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_1991_b65647.json.gz).

Suppose there are 997 points given in a plane. If every two points are joined by a line segment with its midpoint coloured in red, show that there are at least 1991 red points in the plane. Can you find a special case with exactly 1991 red points?


## 1992 — 4

**Record:** `apm_1992_0e43a2`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_1992_0e43a2.json.gz).

Determine all pairs $(h, s)$ of positive integers with the following property: If one draws $h$ horizontal lines and another $s$ lines which satisfy
(i) they are not horizontal,
(ii) no two of them are parallel,
(iii) no three of the $h+s$ lines are concurrent,
then the number of regions formed by these $h+s$ lines is $1992$.

Answer: $(995,1),(176,10)$, and $(80,21)$.


## 1993 — 5

**Record:** `apm_1993_2c32ee`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_1993_2c32ee.json.gz).

Let $P_{1}, P_{2}, \ldots, P_{1993}=P_{0}$ be distinct points in the $xy$-plane with the following properties:
(i) both coordinates of $P_{i}$ are integers, for $i=1,2, \ldots, 1993$;
(ii) there is no point other than $P_{i}$ and $P_{i+1}$ on the line segment joining $P_{i}$ with $P_{i+1}$ whose coordinates are both integers, for $i=0,1, \ldots, 1992$.
Prove that for some $i, 0 \leq i \leq 1992$, there exists a point $Q$ with coordinates ($q_{x}, q_{y}$) on the line segment joining $P_{i}$ with $P_{i+1}$ such that both $2q_{x}$ and $2q_{y}$ are odd integers.


## 1993 — 1

**Record:** `apm_1993_f901a7`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_1993_f901a7.json.gz).

Let $ABCD$ be a quadrilateral such that all sides have equal length and angle $\angle ABC$ is $60$ degrees. Let $\ell$ be a line passing through $D$ and not intersecting the quadrilateral (except at $D$). Let $E$ and $F$ be the points of intersection of $\ell$ with $AB$ and $BC$ respectively. Let $M$ be the point of intersection of $CE$ and $AF$.
Prove that $CA^{2} = CM \times CE$.


## 1994 — 4

**Record:** `apm_1994_74e082`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_1994_74e082.json.gz).

Is there an infinite set of points in the plane such that no three points are collinear, and the distance between any two points is rational?


## 1994 — 2

**Record:** `apm_1994_82c5be`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_1994_82c5be.json.gz).

Given a nondegenerate triangle $A B C$, with circumcentre $O$, orthocentre $H$, and circumradius $R$, prove that $|O H|<3 R$.


## 1999 — 5

**Record:** `apm_1999_563b73`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_1999_563b73.json.gz).

Let $S$ be a set of $2n+1$ points in the plane such that no three are collinear and no four concyclic. A circle will be called good if it has 3 points of $S$ on its circumference, $n-1$ points in its interior and $n-1$ in its exterior. Prove that the number of good circles has the same parity as $n$.


## 2003 — 2

**Record:** `apm_2003_423680`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2003_423680.json.gz).

Suppose $ABCD$ is a square piece of cardboard with side length $a$. On a plane are two parallel lines $\ell_{1}$ and $\ell_{2}$, which are also $a$ units apart. The square $ABCD$ is placed on the plane so that sides $AB$ and $AD$ intersect $\ell_{1}$ at $E$ and $F$ respectively. Also, sides $CB$ and $CD$ intersect $\ell_{2}$ at $G$ and $H$ respectively. Let the perimeters of $\triangle AEF$ and $\triangle CGH$ be $m_{1}$ and $m_{2}$ respectively. Prove that no matter how the square was placed, $m_{1}+m_{2}$ remains constant.


## 2003 — 4

**Record:** `apm_2003_7dec2d`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2003_7dec2d.json.gz).

Let $a$, $b$, $c$ be the sides of a triangle, with $a + b + c = 1$, and let $n \geq 2$ be an integer. Show that
$$
\sqrt[n]{a^{n} + b^{n}} + \sqrt[n]{b^{n} + c^{n}} + \sqrt[n]{c^{n} + a^{n}} < 1 + \frac{\sqrt[n]{2}}{2}
$$


## 2004 — 3

**Record:** `apm_2004_f5148e`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2004_f5148e.json.gz).

Let a set $S$ of 2004 points in the plane be given, no three of which are collinear. Let $\mathcal{L}$ denote the set of all lines (extended indefinitely in both directions) determined by pairs of points from the set. Show that it is possible to colour the points of $S$ with at most two colours, such that for any points $p, q$ of $S$, the number of lines in $\mathcal{L}$ which separate $p$ from $q$ is odd if and only if $p$ and $q$ have the same colour.

Note: A line $\ell$ separates two points $p$ and $q$ if $p$ and $q$ lie on opposite sides of $\ell$ with neither point on $\ell$.


## 2005 — Problem 3

**Record:** `apm_2005_252bb2`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2005_252bb2.json.gz).

Prove that there exists a triangle which can be cut into 2005 congruent triangles.


## 2005 — Problem 5

**Record:** `apm_2005_7d0af9`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2005_7d0af9.json.gz).

In a triangle $A B C$, points $M$ and $N$ are on sides $A B$ and $A C$, respectively, such that $M B = B C = C N$. Let $R$ and $r$ denote the circumradius and the inradius of the triangle $A B C$, respectively. Express the ratio $M N / B C$ in terms of $R$ and $r$.


## 2007 — Problem 3

**Record:** `apm_2007_7a497c`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2007_7a497c.json.gz).

Consider $n$ disks $C_{1}, C_{2}, \ldots, C_{n}$ in a plane such that for each $1 \leq i < n$, the center of $C_{i}$ is on the circumference of $C_{i+1}$, and the center of $C_{n}$ is on the circumference of $C_{1}$. Define the score of such an arrangement of $n$ disks to be the number of pairs $(i, j)$ for which $C_{i}$ properly contains $C_{j}$. Determine the maximum possible score.


## 2008 — Problem 3

**Record:** `apm_2008_6c84cd`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2008_6c84cd.json.gz).

Let $\Gamma$ be the circumcircle of a triangle $A B C$. A circle passing through points $A$ and $C$ meets the sides $B C$ and $B A$ at $D$ and $E$, respectively. The lines $A D$ and $C E$ meet $\Gamma$ again at $G$ and $H$, respectively. The tangent lines of $\Gamma$ at $A$ and $C$ meet the line $D E$ at $L$ and $M$, respectively. Prove that the lines $L H$ and $M G$ meet at $\Gamma$.


## 2008 — Problem 1

**Record:** `apm_2008_f38549`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2008_f38549.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be a triangle with $\angle A < 60^{\circ}$. Let $X$ and $Y$ be the points on the sides $AB$ and $AC$, respectively, such that $CA + AX = CB + BX$ and $BA + AY = BC + CY$. Let $P$ be the point in the plane such that the lines $PX$ and $PY$ are perpendicular to $AB$ and $AC$, respectively. Prove that $\angle BPC < 120^{\circ}$.


## 2010 — 1

**Record:** `apm_2010_4c0e80`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2010_4c0e80.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be a triangle with $\angle BAC \neq 90^{\circ}$. Let $O$ be the circumcenter of the triangle $ABC$ and let $\Gamma$ be the circumcircle of the triangle $BOC$. Suppose that $\Gamma$ intersects the line segment $AB$ at $P$ different from $B$, and the line segment $AC$ at $Q$ different from $C$. Let $ON$ be a diameter of the circle $\Gamma$. Prove that the quadrilateral $APNQ$ is a parallelogram.


## 2010 — 4

**Record:** `apm_2010_de8361`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2010_de8361.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be an acute triangle satisfying the condition $AB > BC$ and $AC > BC$. Denote by $O$ and $H$ the circumcenter and the orthocenter, respectively, of the triangle $ABC$. Suppose that the circumcircle of the triangle $AHC$ intersects the line $AB$ at $M$ different from $A$, and that the circumcircle of the triangle $AHB$ intersects the line $AC$ at $N$ different from $A$. Prove that the circumcenter of the triangle $MNH$ lies on the line $OH$.


## 2013 — Problem 1

**Record:** `apm_2013_4c0b59`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2013_4c0b59.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be an acute triangle with altitudes $AD$, $BE$ and $CF$, and let $O$ be the center of its circumcircle. Show that the segments $OA$, $OF$, $OB$, $OD$, $OC$, $OE$ dissect the triangle $ABC$ into three pairs of triangles that have equal areas.


## 2013 — Problem 5

**Record:** `apm_2013_8a2e44`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2013_8a2e44.json.gz).

Let $ABCD$ be a quadrilateral inscribed in a circle $\omega$, and let $P$ be a point on the extension of $AC$ such that $PB$ and $PD$ are tangent to $\omega$. The tangent at $C$ intersects $PD$ at $Q$ and the line $AD$ at $R$. Let $E$ be the second point of intersection between $AQ$ and $\omega$. Prove that $B$, $E$, $R$ are collinear.


## 2016 — 1

**Record:** `apm_2016_703ac5`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2016_703ac5.json.gz).

We say that a triangle $ABC$ is great if the following holds: for any point $D$ on the side $BC$, if $P$ and $Q$ are the feet of the perpendiculars from $D$ to the lines $AB$ and $AC$, respectively, then the reflection of $D$ in the line $PQ$ lies on the circumcircle of the triangle $ABC$.
Prove that triangle $ABC$ is great if and only if $\angle A = 90^{\circ}$ and $AB = AC$.


## 2016 — 3

**Record:** `apm_2016_7112d6`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2016_7112d6.json.gz).

Let $AB$ and $AC$ be two distinct rays not lying on the same line, and let $\omega$ be a circle with center $O$ that is tangent to ray $AC$ at $E$ and ray $AB$ at $F$. Let $R$ be a point on segment $EF$. The line through $O$ parallel to $EF$ intersects line $AB$ at $P$. Let $N$ be the intersection of lines $PR$ and $AC$, and let $M$ be the intersection of line $AB$ and the line through $R$ parallel to $AC$. Prove that line $MN$ is tangent to $\omega$.


## 2018 — 3

**Record:** `mkd_2018_77a86a`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/north-macedonia/mkd_2018_77a86a.json.gz).

A collection of $n$ squares on the plane is called tri-connected if the following criteria are satisfied:
(i) All the squares are congruent
(ii) If two squares have a point $P$ in common, then $P$ is a vertex of each of the squares.
(iii) Each square touches exactly three other squares.
How many positive integers $n$ are there with $2018 \le n \le 3018$ such that there exists a collection of $n$ squares that is tri-connected?


## 2018 — 1

**Record:** `mkd_2018_8e711e`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/north-macedonia/mkd_2018_8e711e.json.gz).

Let $H$ be the orthocenter of triangle $ABC$. Let $M$ and $N$ be the midpoints of the sides $AB$ and $AC$ respectively. Assume that $H$ lies inside the quadrilateral $BMNC$ and that the circumcircles of triangles $BMH$ and $CNH$ are tangent to each other. The line through $H$ parallel to $BC$ intersects the circumcircles of the triangles $BMH$ and $CNH$ in the points $K$ and $L$, respectively. Let $F$ be the intersection point of $MK$ and $NL$ and let $J$ be the incenter of triangle $MHN$. Prove that $FJ = FA$.


## 2018 — 4

**Record:** `mkd_2018_8ede86`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/north-macedonia/mkd_2018_8ede86.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be an equilateral triangle. From the vertex $A$ we draw a ray towards the interior of the triangle such that the ray reaches one of the sides of the triangle. When the ray reaches a side, it then bounces off following the law of reflection, that is, if it arrives with a directed angle $\alpha$, it leaves with a directed angle $180^\circ - \alpha$. After $n$ bounces, the ray returns to $A$ without ever landing on any of the other vertices. Find all possible values of $n$.


## 2020 — 1

**Record:** `apm_2020_1a86f9`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2020_1a86f9.json.gz).

Let $\Gamma$ be the circumcircle of $\triangle A B C$. Let $D$ be a point on the side $B C$. The tangent to $\Gamma$ at $A$ intersects the parallel line to $B A$ through $D$ at point $E$. The segment $C E$ intersects $\Gamma$ again at $F$. Suppose $B, D, F, E$ are concyclic. Prove that $A C, B F, D E$ are concurrent.


## 2023 — 5

**Record:** `apm_2023_effff8`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2023_effff8.json.gz).

There are $n$ line segments on the plane, no three intersecting at a point, and each pair intersecting once in their respective interiors. Tony and his $2 n-1$ friends each stand at a distinct endpoint of a line segment. Tony wishes to send Christmas presents to each of his friends as follows:
First, he chooses an endpoint of each segment as a "sink". Then he places the present at the endpoint of the segment he is at. The present moves as follows:
- If it is on a line segment, it moves towards the sink.
- When it reaches an intersection of two segments, it changes the line segment it travels on and starts moving towards the new sink.
If the present reaches an endpoint, the friend on that endpoint can receive their present. Prove Tony can send presents to exactly $n$ of his $2 n-1$ friends.


## 2023 — 1

**Record:** `apm_2023_fb45d1`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2023_fb45d1.json.gz).

Let $n \geq 5$ be an integer. Consider $n$ squares with side lengths $1,2, \ldots, n$, respectively. The squares are arranged in the plane with their sides parallel to the $x$ and $y$ axes. Suppose that no two squares touch, except possibly at their vertices.
Show that it is possible to arrange these squares in a way such that every square touches exactly two other squares.


## 2024 — 1

**Record:** `apm_2024_455746`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2024_455746.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be an acute triangle. Let $D$ be a point on side $AB$ and $E$ be a point on side $AC$ such that lines $BC$ and $DE$ are parallel. Let $X$ be an interior point of $BCED$. Suppose rays $DX$ and $EX$ meet side $BC$ at points $P$ and $Q$, respectively, such that both $P$ and $Q$ lie between $B$ and $C$. Suppose that the circumcircles of triangles $BQX$ and $CPX$ intersect at a point $Y \neq X$. Prove that points $A$, $X$, and $Y$ are collinear.


## 2024 — 5

**Record:** `apm_2024_5b1ccf`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2024_5b1ccf.json.gz).

Line $\ell$ intersects sides $B C$ and $A D$ of cyclic quadrilateral $A B C D$ in its interior points $R$ and $S$ respectively, and intersects ray $D C$ beyond point $C$ at $Q$, and ray $B A$ beyond point $A$ at $P$. Circumcircles of the triangles $Q C R$ and $Q D S$ intersect at $N \neq Q$, while circumcircles of the triangles $P A S$ and $P B R$ intersect at $M \neq P$. Let lines $M P$ and $N Q$ meet at point $X$, lines $A B$ and $C D$ meet at point $K$ and lines $B C$ and $A D$ meet at point $L$. Prove that point $X$ lies on line $K L$.


## 2025 — 2

**Record:** `apm_2025_0453f0`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/asia-pacific-mathematics-olympiad-apmo/apm_2025_0453f0.json.gz).

Let $\alpha$ and $\beta$ be positive real numbers. Emerald makes a trip in the coordinate plane, starting off from the origin $(0,0)$. Each minute she moves one unit up or one unit to the right, restricting herself to the region $|x-y|<2025$, in the coordinate plane. By the time she visits a point ( $x, y$ ) she writes down the integer $\lfloor x \alpha+y \beta\rfloor$ on it. It turns out that Emerald wrote each non-negative integer exactly once. Find all the possible pairs $(\alpha, \beta)$ for which such a trip would be possible.

## 2004 — 3.

**Record:** `rou_2004_6e709c`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romania/rou_2004_6e709c.json.gz).

Fie $O$ un punct interior triunghiului ascuţitunghic $ABC$. Cercurile centrate în mijloacele laturilor triunghiului şi care trec prin $O$, se intersectează a doua oară în $K$, $L$ şi $M$.
Demonstraţi că $O$ este centrul cercului înscris în triunghiul $KLM$ dacă şi numai dacă $O$ este centrul cercului circumscris triunghiului $ABC$.


## 2004 — 4.

**Record:** `rou_2004_775bce`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romania/rou_2004_775bce.json.gz).

O mulţime finită de drepte, oricare trei neconcurente, partiţionează planul într-un număr de regiuni. Două regiuni se numesc "vecine" dacă frontierele lor au în comun mai mult decât un segment nedegenerat.

În fiecare regiune trebuie scris un număr întreg astfel încât:
(i) produsul numerelor scrise în oricare două regiuni vecine este strict mai mic decât suma lor;
(ii) pentru fiecare dintre dreptele date, suma numerelor scrise în regiunile aflate de aceeaşi parte a ei este egală cu $0$.

Demonstraţi că acesta este posibil dacă şi numai dacă nu toate dreptele sunt paralele.


## 2004 — Problema 1

**Record:** `rou_2004_f162c5`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romania/rou_2004_f162c5.json.gz).

Fie $I$ centrul cercului înscris în triunghiul $ABC$ şi $A_{1}$, $B_{1}$ şi $C_{1}$ puncte arbitrare pe segmentele $(AI)$, $(BI)$, respectiv $(CI)$. Mediatoarele segmentelor $AA_{1}$, $BB_{1}$ şi $CC_{1}$ se intersectează în $A_{2}$, $B_{2}$ şi respectiv $C_{2}$. Arătați că centrul cercului circumscris triunghiului $A_{2}B_{2}C_{2}$ coincide cu centrul cercului circumscris triunghiului $ABC$ dacă şi numai dacă $I$ este ortocentrul triunghiului $A_{1}B_{1}C_{1}$.

**Record:** `mkd_2010_6b8e73`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/north-macedonia/mkd_2010_6b8e73.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be an acute triangle with orthocenter $H$, and let $M$ be the midpoint of $AC$. The point $C_1$ on $AB$ is such that $CC_1$ is an altitude of the triangle $ABC$. Let $H_1$ be the reflection of $H$ in $AB$. The orthogonal projections of $C_1$ onto the lines $AH_1$, $AC$ and $BC$ are $P$, $Q$ and $R$, respectively. Let $M_1$ be the point such that the circumcentre of triangle $PQR$ is the midpoint of the segment $MM_1$.
Prove that $M_1$ lies on the segment $BH_1$.


## 2010 — Problem 3.

**Record:** `mkd_2010_b41ce7`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/north-macedonia/mkd_2010_b41ce7.json.gz).

A strip of width $w$ is the set of all points which lie on, or between, two parallel lines distance $w$ apart. Let $S$ be a set of $n$ ($n \ge 3$) points on the plane such that any three different points of $S$ can be covered by a strip of width $1$.
Prove that $S$ can be covered by a strip of width $2$.


## 2010 — Problem 3.

**Record:** `rou_2010_b512fc`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romania/rou_2010_b512fc.json.gz).

A strip of breadth $\ell$ is the set of those points in the plane, situated between or on two parallel lines at distance $\ell$ one from the other.
Consider a finite set $S$ of $n \geq 3$ (distinct) points in the plane, with the property that any three points of $S$ can be covered by a strip of breadth 1. Prove the set $S$ can be covered by a strip of breadth 2.


## 2011 — 4

**Record:** `mkd_2011_b4aec1`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/north-macedonia/mkd_2011_b4aec1.json.gz).

Let $ABCDEF$ be a convex hexagon of area $1$ whose opposite sides are parallel. The lines $AB$, $CD$ and $EF$ meet in pairs to determine the vertices of a triangle. Similarly, the lines $BC$, $DA$ and $FA$ meet in pairs to determine the vertices of another triangle. Show that the area of the least one of these two triangles is at least $\frac{3}{2}$.


## 2012 — 2

**Record:** `mkd_2012_857951`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/north-macedonia/mkd_2012_857951.json.gz).

$$
\sum_{\text{cyc}} (x+y)\sqrt{(z+x)(z+y)} \ge 4(xy+yz+zx)
$$
for all positive real numbers $x$, $y$, $z$.


## 2012 — 1

**Record:** `mkd_2012_d5746f`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/north-macedonia/mkd_2012_d5746f.json.gz).

Let $A$, $B$, $C$ be points lying on a circle $\Gamma$ with the center $O$ and assume that $\angle ABC > 90^\circ$. Let $D$ be the point of intersection of the line $AB$ and the line perpendicular to $AC$ at $C$. Let $l$ be the line through $D$ and perpendicular to $AO$. Let $E$ be the point of intersection of $l$ and the line $AC$, and $F$ be the point of intersection of $\Gamma$ and $l$ that lies between $D$ and $E$. Prove that the circumcircles of the triangles $BFE$ and $CFD$ are tangent at $F$.


## 2013 — Problem 1.

**Record:** `grc_2013_d207eb`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/greece/grc_2013_d207eb.json.gz).

In a triangle $ABC$, the excircle $\omega_a$ opposite $A$ touches $AB$ at $P$ and $AC$ at $Q$, and the excircle $\omega_b$ opposite $B$ touches $BA$ at $M$ and $BC$ at $N$. Let $K$ be the projection of $C$ onto $MN$, and let $L$ be the projection of $C$ onto $PQ$. Show that the quadrilateral $MKLP$ is cyclic.


## 2014 — 4

**Record:** `mkd_2014_9ea0c9`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/north-macedonia/mkd_2014_9ea0c9.json.gz).

Let $n$ be a positive integer. A regular hexagon with side length $n$ is divided into equilateral triangles with side length $1$ by lines parallel to its sides.
Find the number of regular hexagons all of whose vertices are along the vertices of the equilateral triangles.


## 2018 — 1

**Record:** `mkd_2018_85c0d3`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/north-macedonia/mkd_2018_85c0d3.json.gz).

A quadrilateral $ABCD$ is inscribed in a circle $k$, where $AB > CD$ and $AB$ is not parallel to $CD$. Point $M$ is the intersection of the diagonals $AC$ and $BD$ and the perpendicular from $M$ to $AB$ intersects the segment $AB$ at the point $E$. If $EM$ bisects the angle $CED$, prove that $AB$ is a diameter of the circle $k$.


## 2018 — 2

**Record:** `mkd_2018_881ec5`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/north-macedonia/mkd_2018_881ec5.json.gz).

Let $q$ be a positive rational number. Two ants are initially at the same point $X$ in the plane. In the $n$-th minute ($n=1,2,3,\ldots$) each of them chooses whether to walk due north, east, south or west and then walks the distance of $q^n$ meter. After a whole number of minutes, they are at the same point in the plane (not necessarily $X$), but have not taken exactly the same route within that time. Determine all possible values of $q$.



# BMO-SL source statements

## 2010 — G2

**Record:** `bmo_2010_14ebeb`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2010_14ebeb.json.gz).

Consider a cyclic quadrilateral such that the midpoints of its sides form another cyclic quadrilateral. Prove that the area of the smaller circle is less than or equal to half the area of the bigger circle.


## 2010 — G3

**Record:** `bmo_2010_50813e`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2010_50813e.json.gz).

The incircle of a triangle $A_0B_0C_0$ touches the sides $B_0C_0$, $C_0A_0$, $A_0B_0$ at the points $A$, $B$, $C$, respectively, and the incircle of the triangle $ABC$ with incenter $I$ touches the sides $BC$, $CA$, $AB$ at the points $A_1$, $B_1$, $C_1$, respectively. Let $\sigma(ABC)$ and $\sigma(A_1B_1C)$ be the areas of the triangles $ABC$ and $A_1B_1C$ respectively. Show that if $\sigma(ABC) = 2\sigma(A_1B_1C)$, then the lines $AA_0$, $BB_0$, $IC_1$ pass through a common point.


## 2010 — G7

**Record:** `bmo_2010_a08034`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2010_a08034.json.gz).

A triangle $ABC$ is given. Let $M$ be the midpoint of the side $AC$ of the triangle and $Z$ the image of point $B$ along the line $BM$. The circle with center $M$ and radius $MB$ intersects the lines $BA$ and $BC$ at the points $E$ and $G$ respectively. Let $H$ be the point of intersection of $EG$ with the line $AC$, and $K$ the point of intersection of $HZ$ with the line $EB$. The perpendicular from point $K$ to the line $BH$, intersects the lines $BZ$ and $BH$ at the points $L$ and $N$, respectively.
If $P$ is the second point of intersection of the circumscribed circles of the triangles $KZL$ and $BLN$, prove that the lines $BZ$, $KN$ and $HP$ intersect at a common point.


## 2010 — G8

**Record:** `bmo_2010_a8a66d`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2010_a8a66d.json.gz).

Let $c(O, R)$ be a circle with diameter $AB$ and $C$ a point on it different than $A$ and $B$ such that $\angle AOC > 90^\circ$. On the radius $OC$ we consider the point $K$ and the circle ($c_1$) with center $K$ and radius $KC = R_1$. We draw the tangents $AD$ and $AE$ from $A$ to the circle ($c_1$). Prove that the straight lines $AC$, $BK$ and $DE$ are concurrent.


## 2010 — G4

**Record:** `bmo_2010_cfb901`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2010_cfb901.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be a given triangle and $l$ be a line that meets the lines $BC$, $CA$ and $AB$ in $A_1$, $B_1$ and $C_1$ respectively. Let $A'$ be the midpoint of the segment connecting the projections of $A_1$ onto the lines $AB$ and $AC$. Construct analogously the points $B'$ and $C'$.

a. Show that the points $A'$, $B'$ and $C'$ are collinear on some line $l'$.

b. Show that if $l$ contains the circumcenter of the triangle $ABC$, then $l'$ contains the center of its Euler circle.


## 2010 — C3

**Record:** `bmo_2010_d8caea`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2010_d8caea.json.gz).

By a *strip* of breadth $b$ we mean a closed part of the plane consisting of all points that lie between two parallel lines at distance $b$ from each other. Let $S$ be a finite set of $n$ ($n \ge 4$) points in the plane, such that any three points from $S$ can be covered by a strip of breadth 1. Prove that $S$ can be covered by a strip of breadth 2.


## 2012 — A4

**Record:** `bmo_2012_0ec531`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2012_0ec531.json.gz).

Let $ABCD$ be a square in the plane $P$. Find the minimum and the maximum values of the function $f: P \to \mathbb{R}$ defined by
$$
f(P) = \frac{PA + PB}{PC + PD}
$$
where $\mathbb{R}$ is the set of all real numbers.


## 2012 — G6

**Record:** `bmo_2012_3d1c38`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2012_3d1c38.json.gz).

Let $P$ and $Q$ be points inside a triangle $ABC$ such that $\angle PAC = \angle QAB$ and $\angle PBC = \angle QBA$. Let $D$ and $E$ be the feet of the perpendiculars from $P$ to the lines $BC$ and $AC$, and $F$ be the foot of perpendicular from $Q$ to the line $AB$. Let $M$ be the intersection of the lines $DE$ and $AB$. Prove that $MP \perp CF$.


## 2012 — G3

**Record:** `bmo_2012_70f90f`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2012_70f90f.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be a triangle with circumcircle $c$ and circumcenter $O$, and let $D$ be a point on the side $BC$ different from the vertices and the midpoint of $BC$. Let $K$ be the point where the circumcircle $c_1$ of the triangle $BOD$ intersects $c$ for the second time and let $Z$ be the point where $c_1$ meets the line $AB$. Let $M$ be the point where the circumcircle $c_2$ of the triangle $COD$ intersects $c$ for the second time and let $E$ be the point where $c_2$ meets the line $AC$. Finally let $N$ be the point where the circumcircle $c_3$ of the triangle $AEZ$ meets $c$ again. Prove that the triangles $ABC$ and $NKM$ are congruent.


## 2012 — G2

**Record:** `bmo_2012_bfceb3`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2012_bfceb3.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be a triangle, $O$ its circumcenter and $AD$ the bisector of the angle $A$ where $D \in BC$. Let $\ell$ be the line passing through $O$ and parallel to the bisector $AD$. Prove that $\ell$ passes through the orthocenter $H$ of the triangle $ABC$ if and only if $ABC$ is isosceles or $\angle BAC = 120^{\circ}$.


## 2012 — G5

**Record:** `bmo_2012_d294bd`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2012_d294bd.json.gz).

The incircle of a triangle $ABC$ touches its sides $BC$, $CA$, $AB$ at the points $A_1$, $B_1$, $C_1$, respectively. Let the projections of the orthocenter $H_1$ of the triangle $A_1B_1C_1$ to the lines $AA_1$ and $BC$ be $P$ and $Q$, respectively. Show that the line $PQ$ bisects the line segment $B_1C_1$.


## 2012 — G4

**Record:** `bmo_2012_e25616`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2012_e25616.json.gz).

Let $M$ be the point of intersection of the diagonals of a cyclic quadrilateral $ABCD$. Let $I_1$ and $I_2$ be the incenters of triangles $AMD$ and $BMC$, respectively, and let $L$ be the point of intersection of the lines $DI_1$ and $CI_2$. The foot of the perpendicular from the midpoint $T$ of $I_1I_2$ to $CL$ is $N$, and $F$ is the midpoint of $TN$. Let $G$ and $J$ be the points of intersection of the line $LF$ with $I_1N$ and $I_1I_2$, respectively. Let $O_1$ be the circumcenter of triangle $LI_1J$, and let $\Gamma_1$ and $\Gamma_2$ be the circles with diameters $O_1L$ and $O_1J$, respectively. Let $V$ and $S$ be the second points of intersection of $I_1O_1$ with $\Gamma_1$ and $\Gamma_2$, respectively. If $K$ is the point where the circles $\Gamma_1$ and $\Gamma_2$ meet again, prove that $K$ is the circumcenter of the triangle $SVG$.


## 2014 — G7

**Record:** `bmo_2014_1d88b3`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2014_1d88b3.json.gz).

Let $I$ be the incenter of $\triangle ABC$ and let $H_a$, $H_b$, and $H_c$ be the orthocenters of $\triangle BIC$, $\triangle CIA$, and $\triangle AIB$, respectively. The line $H_aH_b$ meets $AB$ at $X$ and the line $H_aH_c$ meets $AC$ at $Y$. ## 2014 — G4

**Record:** `bmo_2014_2a985d`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2014_2a985d.json.gz).

Let $A_0B_0C_0$ be a triangle with area equal to $\sqrt{2}$. We consider the excenters $A_1$, $B_1$ and $C_1$ then we consider the excenters, say $A_2$, $B_2$ and $C_2$, of the triangle $A_1B_1C_1$. By continuing this procedure, examine if it is possible to arrive to a triangle $A_nB_nC_n$ with all coordinates rational.

**Record:** `bmo_2014_35e42c`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2014_35e42c.json.gz).

Prove that there exist infinitely many non isosceles triangles with rational side lengths, rational lengths of altitudes, and perimeter equal to $3$.

## 2014 — G3

**Record:** `bmo_2014_4dd9ae`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2014_4dd9ae.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be an isosceles triangle, ($AB = AC$). Let $D$ and $E$ be two points on the side $BC$ such that $D \in BE$, $E \in DC$ and $2\angle DAE = \angle BAC$. Prove that we can construct a triangle $XYZ$ such that $XY = BD$, $YZ = DE$ and $ZX = EC$. Find $\angle BAC + \angle YXZ$.


## 2014 — C2

**Record:** `bmo_2014_5daa9c`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2014_5daa9c.json.gz).

Let $M = \{1, 2, \dots, 2013\}$ and let $\Gamma$ be a circle. For every nonempty subset $\mathcal{A}$ of the set $M$, denote by $S(\mathcal{A})$ the sum of elements of the set $\mathcal{A}$, and define $S(\emptyset) = 0$ ($\emptyset$ is the empty set). Is it possible to join every subset $\mathcal{A}$ of $M$ with some point $A$ on the circle $\Gamma$ so that following conditions are fulfilled:
1. Different subsets are joined with different points;
2. All joined points are vertices of a regular polygon;
3. If $A_1, A_2, \dots, A_k$ are some of the joined points, $k > 2$, such that $A_1A_2 \dots A_k$ is a regular $k$-gon, then 2014 divides $S(\mathcal{A}_1) + S(\mathcal{A}_2) + \dots + S(\mathcal{A}_k)$?


## 2014 — C3

**Record:** `bmo_2014_96bc5b`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2014_96bc5b.json.gz).

Consider a lattice of side length $1$ equilateral triangles forming a regular hexagon of side length $n$. Show that the number of ways of simultaneously selecting six vertices of the lattice to form the vertices of a regular hexagon is a perfect square.

If the line, perpendicular to $BD$ at $E$, intersects $CD$ at $M$, prove that $KM \perp DL$.


## 2015 — G6.

Let $AB$ be a diameter of a circle ($\omega$) with centre $O$. Let $ABC$ be a triangle and $C$ its circumcircle. The point $D$ lies on the arc $BC$ of $C$ and is different from $B$, $C$ and the midpoint of $BC$. The tangent line to $C$ at $D$ intersects the lines $BC$, $CA$, $AB$ at $A'$, $B'$, $C'$, respectively. The lines $BB'$ and $CC'$ intersect at $E$. In the acute angled triangle $ABC$ consider the altitudes $BB'$ and $CC'$. The half-line $C'B'$ intersects the circumcircle of triangle at $B''$ and denote by $\alpha_A$ the angle $\widehat{ABB''}$. In a similar way define the angles $\alpha_B$ and $\alpha_C$. Prove the inequality

**Record:** `bmo_2015_c975a8`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2015_c975a8.json.gz).

## 2017 — C4

**Record:** `bmo_2017_1f4aba`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2017_1f4aba.json.gz).

For any set of points $A_1, A_2, ..., A_n$ on the plane, one defines $r(A_1, A_2, ..., A_n)$ as the radius of the smallest circle that contains all of these points. Prove that if $n \ge 3$, there are indices $i, j, k$ such that
$$
r(A_1, A_2, ..., A_n) = r(A_i, A_j, A_k).
$$

$$
and

## 2017 — A4

**Record:** `bmo_2017_f27d27`. **Category:** shortlist. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/balkan-mathematical-olympiad/bmo_2017_f27d27.json.gz).

Let $M = \{(a,b,c) \in \mathbb{R}^3: 0 < a, b, c < \frac{1}{2} \text{ with } a+b+c=1\}$ and $f: M \to \mathbb{R}$ given as
$$
f(a, b, c) = 4\left(\frac{1}{a} + \frac{1}{b} + \frac{1}{c}\right) - \frac{1}{abc}
$$
Find the best (real) bounds $\alpha$ and $\beta$ such that
$$
f(M) = \{f(a, b, c) : (a, b, c) \in M\} \subseteq [\alpha, \beta]
$$
and determine whether any of them is achievable.

We will deem certain positive integer numbers to be *good* with the condition that if the lengths of two sides of a super-integer triangle are two (not necessarily different) good numbers, then the length of the remaining side is also a good number. Let $5$ be a good number. Prove that all integers larger than $2$ are good numbers.

Let $\triangle ABC$ be a triangle with $\angle BAC = 2\angle CBA$ and $AB < AC$. A point $P$ lies on segment $AC$ such that $PC = AB + AP$. Let $O$ be the circumcentre of triangle $\triangle ABP$, and let the line through $O$ parallel to $AB$ intersect $BP$ at $Q$. Show that $AQ$ passes through the midpoint of segment $BC$.


## 2025 — G5

Let $ABCD$ be a convex quadrilateral such that $AB^2 + BC^2 = AD^2 + CD^2$. Points $X$ and $Y$ are chosen such that $XD \perp CD$, $XB \perp AB$, $YB \perp BC$ and $YD \perp AD$. Let lines $AC$ and $XY$ meet at $T$ and $M$ be the midpoint of segment $XY$. Prove that points $T, M, B, D$ lie on a circle.



# CMO-China source statements

## 2022 — 3

**Record:** `chn_2022_aece1e`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/china/chn_2022_aece1e.json.gz).

Fix two positive integers $m$ and $n$. Fix a way to color the vertices of a regular $(2m+2n)$-gon so that $2m$ of them are black and the other $2n$ are white. Define the coloring distance $d(B, C)$ between two black points $B$ and $C$ to be the lesser of the numbers of white points on either side of the line $BC$; similarly, define the coloring distance $d(W, X)$ between two white points $W$ and $X$ to be the lesser of the numbers of black points on either side of the line $WX$.
A black pairing scheme $\mathcal{B}$ means to label the $2m$ black points as $B_1, \dots, B_m, C_1, \dots, C_m$, such that no two segments from $B_1C_1, \dots, B_mC_m$ intersect each other. For each such $\mathcal{B}$, put
$$
P(\mathcal{B}) = \sum_{i=1}^{m} d(B_i, C_i).
$$
A white pairing scheme $\mathcal{W}$ means to label the $2n$ white points as $W_1, \dots, W_n, X_1, \dots, X_n$, such that no two segments from $W_1X_1, \dots, W_nX_n$ intersect each other. For each such $\mathcal{W}$, put
$$
P(\mathcal{W}) = \sum_{i=1}^{n} d(W_i, X_i).
$$
Prove that, regardless of how the $2m+2n$ vertices are colored, we always have
$$
\max_{\mathcal{B}} P(\mathcal{B}) = \max_{\mathcal{W}} P(\mathcal{W}),
$$
where the maxima are taken over all possible black pairing schemes and all possible white pairing schemes, respectively.


## 2023 — 5

**Record:** `chn_2023_ca3b99`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/china/chn_2023_ca3b99.json.gz).

Let $\triangle ABC$ be an acute triangle, and let $K$ be a point on the extension ray of $BC$ (so that $C$ lies on the segment $BK$). Let $P$ be a point such that $BP = BK$ and $PK \parallel AB$, and let $Q$ be a point such that $CQ = CK$ and $QK \parallel AC$. Assume that the circumcircle of $\triangle PQK$ and the line $AK$ intersects at another point $T$.

(1) Prove that $\angle APB + \angle BTC = \angle CQA$.

(2) Prove that $AP \cdot BT \cdot CQ = AQ \cdot BP \cdot CT$.

**Record:** `chn_2014_fcf4c1`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/china/chn_2014_fcf4c1.json.gz).

Let $O$ be the circumcenter of triangle $ABC$. $H_A$ is the projection of $A$ onto $BC$. The extension of $AO$ intersects the circumcircle of $BOC$ at $A'$. The projections of $A'$ onto $AB$, $AC$ are $D$, $E$, and $O_A$ is the circumcenter of triangle $DH_AE$. Define $H_B, O_B, H_C, O_C$ similarly.
Prove: $H_AO_A$, $H_BO_B$, $H_CO_C$ are concurrent.

**Record:** `chn_2021_0b68e6`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/china/chn_2021_0b68e6.json.gz).

Find the largest constant $C > 0$, such that for any integer $n \ge 2$, one can find real numbers $x_1, x_2, \dots, x_n \in [-1, 1]$ satisfying
$$
\prod_{1 \le i < j \le n} (x_i - x_j) \ge C^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}.
$$

## 2021 — 1

**Record:** `chn_2021_376391`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/china/chn_2021_376391.json.gz).

Given a convex $n$-gon ($n \ge 5$) $\Omega: P_1P_2...P_n$, of which no three diagonals are concurrent inside $\Omega$. Prove that one can choose a point inside every quadrilateral $P_iP_jP_kP_l$ ($1 \le i < j < k < l \le n$) and not on any diagonal of $\Omega$, such that the $C_n^4$ points obtained are distinct and the segment connecting any two of them intersects with at least one diagonal of $\Omega$.

**Record:** `chn_2021_c29903`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/china/chn_2021_c29903.json.gz).

As illustrated in Fig. 2.1, in the acute $\triangle ABC$, $AB < AC$, $I$ is the incentre, and $\odot O$ is the circumcentre. Let $M$, $N$ be the midpoints of $\widehat{BAC}$ and $\widehat{BC}$, respectively. Let $D$ be a point on $\widehat{AC}$ such that $AD // BC$. The inscribed circle of $\triangle ABC$ against $\angle BAC$ touches $BC$ at $E$. Let $F$ be a point inside $\triangle ABC$, satisfying $IF // BC$ and $\angle BAF = \angle CAE$. The line $NF$ meets $\odot O$ at $R$ other than $N$, the lines $AF$ and $DI$ meet at $K$, and the lines $AR$ and $IF$ meet at $L$.
Prove: $NK \perp ML$.

**Record:** `chn_2021_cb4abc`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/china/chn_2021_cb4abc.json.gz).

Prove that there exists a constant $\lambda > 0$, such that: for any positive integer $m$, in the rectangular coordinates system, if all vertices of $\triangle ABC$ are integral points, and there is a unique interior (not on the side) integral point whose $x$ and $y$ coordinates are multiples of $m$, then the area of $\triangle ABC$ is less than $\lambda m^3$.

(Contributed by Wang Bin and Fu Yunhao)

## 2023 — 1

**Record:** `chn_2023_4ae399`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/china/chn_2023_4ae399.json.gz).

Given two fixed points $A$ and $B$ on the unit circle $\omega$, satisfying $\sqrt{2} < AB < 2$. Let $P$ be a moving point on $\omega$ such that $\triangle ABP$ is an acute-angled triangle and $AP > AB > BP$.

For this moving point $P$, let $H$ be the orthocenter of $\triangle ABP$. Take a point $S$ on the arc $\widehat{AP}$ such that $SH = AH$, and take a point $T$ on the arc $\widehat{AB}$ such that $TB \parallel AP$. Let $Q$ be the intersection of lines $ST$ and $BP$.

Given an odd prime $p$. Find the largest positive integer $n$ such that there exist $n$ integer-coordinate points $A_1, A_2, \dots, A_n$ in the plane, with no three collinear, and for any $1 \le i < j < k \le n$, twice the area of triangle $A_iA_jA_k$ is **not** divisible by $p$.


## 2025 — Problem 9

**Record:** `chn_2025_53da42`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/china/chn_2025_53da42.json.gz).

A finite point set $P = \{(x_i, y_i) \mid i = 1, 2, \dots, n\}$ in the plane is called **sparse** if for any $1 \le i < j \le n$,
$$
|x_i - x_j| \cdot |y_i - y_j| \ge 1.
$$
(1) Let $\{Q_1, Q_2, Q_3\}$ be a sparse set where no $Q_i$ lies inside the axis-aligned rectangle with the other two points as diagonal vertices. Prove that the area of triangle $Q_1Q_2Q_3$ is at least $\frac{\sqrt{5}}{2}$.

(2) Let $P$ be a sparse set with no three collinear points, all lying inside an axis-aligned square of side length $a$. Prove that the number of points in $P$ does not exceed
$$
\frac{a^2}{\sqrt{5}} + 2a + 1.
$$
