Let $D$, $G$ be the reflections of $A$ over $O$ and the circumcenter of triangle $AEF$. Let $K$ be the reflection of $O$ over the circumcenter of triangle $OBC$.

a) Prove that $D$, $G$ and $K$ are collinear.

b) Take $M$ on $KB$ and $N$ on $KC$ such that $IM$ is perpendicular to $AC$ and $IN$ is perpendicular to $AB$. The perpendicular bisector of $IK$ intersects $MN$ at $H$. Suppose that $IH$ meets $AB$, $AC$ at $P$, $Q$ respectively. Prove that the circumcircle of triangle $APQ$ intersects $(O)$ again at a point on $AI$.


## 2023 — 2

**Record:** `chn_2023_b592f6`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/china/chn_2023_b592f6.json.gz).

In acute triangle $ABC$ which is not isosceles, $AP$, $BQ$, $CR$ are three altitudes, $H$ is the orthocenter. The parallel line to $BC$ passing through $A$ intersects line $RQ$ at point $D$. Let $A_1$ be the midpoint of $BC$, and let $K$ be the intersection of $RQ$ and $AA_1$. The line passing through the midpoint of $AH$ and point $K$ intersects line $DA_1$ at point $A_2$. Similarly define points $B_2$ and $C_2$.

Suppose that the circumcircle of non-degenerate triangle $A_2B_2C_2$ is $\omega$. Prove: There exist three circles $\odot A'$, $\odot B'$ and $\odot C'$ inside $\omega$ that are tangent to $\omega$ and satisfy the following conditions:
(1) $\odot A'$ is tangent to sides $AB$, $AC$, $\odot B'$ is tangent to sides $BA$, $BC$, and $\odot C'$ is tangent to sides $CA$, $CB$;
(2) The centers of the three circles, $A'$, $B'$, $C'$, are distinct and collinear.


## 2023 — 5

**Record:** `chn_2023_b8f301`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/china/chn_2023_b8f301.json.gz).

Suppose $P_1, \dots, P_n$ are $n$ points inside triangle $ABC$, such that any three points among $P_1, \dots, P_n, A, B, C$ are not collinear. Prove: It is possible to divide $\triangle ABC$ into a union of $2n+1$ smaller triangles such that each smaller triangle has its vertices among $P_1, \dots, P_n, A, B, C$, and the number of smaller triangles with vertices including at least one point among $A, B, C$ is no less than $n + \sqrt{n} + 1$.


## 2023 — 1

**Record:** `chn_2023_c81d0b`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/china/chn_2023_c81d0b.json.gz).

Suppose $n \ge 2$ is a positive integer, and $A_1A_2 \cdots A_{2n}$ is a convex $2n$-gon inscribed in a circle. It is known that there exists a point $P$ inside this $2n$-gon such that
$$
\angle PA_1A_2 = \angle PA_2A_3 = \cdots = \angle PA_{2n-1}A_{2n} = \angle PA_{2n}A_1.
$$
Prove that the following equation holds
$$
\prod_{i=1}^{n} |A_{2i-1}A_{2i}| = \prod_{i=1}^{n} |A_{2i}A_{2i+1}|,
$$
where $A_{2n+1} = A_1$.


## 2023 — Problem 2

**Record:** `ind_2023_26115d`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/india/ind_2023_26115d.json.gz).

The line through $K$ perpendicular to line $DK$ meets the circle with diameter $BC$ at points $X$ and $Y$. Prove that $AX \cdot DY = DX \cdot AY$.


## 2023 — Problem 1

**Record:** `ind_2023_6c27f2`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/india/ind_2023_6c27f2.json.gz).

Point $L$ is chosen on the internal angle bisector of $\angle BZC$ such that $AL = LI$. Let $M$ be midpoint of arc $BZC$, and let $V$ be the midpoint of $ID$. Prove that $\angle IML = \angle DVM$.


## 2023 — USA TST 2023/1

**Record:** `usa_2023_942b5a`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/united-states/usa_2023_942b5a.json.gz).

There are 2022 equally spaced points on a circular track $\gamma$ of circumference 2022. The points are labeled $A_1, A_2, \dots, A_{2022}$ in some order, each label used once. Initially, Bunbun the Bunny begins at $A_1$. She hops along $\gamma$ from $A_1$ to $A_2$, then from $A_2$ to $A_3$, until she reaches $A_{2022}$, after which she hops back to $A_1$. When hopping from $P$ to $Q$, she always hops along the shorter of the two arcs $\widehat{PQ}$ of $\gamma$; if $\widehat{PQ}$ is a diameter of $\gamma$, she moves along either semicircle.
Determine the maximal possible sum of the lengths of the 2022 arcs which Bunbun traveled, over all possible labellings of the 2022 points.


## 2023 — 5

**Record:** `vnm_2023_030c5c`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2023_030c5c.json.gz).

Let $ABCD$ be a convex quadrilateral with $\angle B < \angle A < 90^\circ$. Let $I$ be the midpoint of $AB$ and $S$ the intersection of $AD$ and $BC$. Suppose that $EF$ intersects the circumcircle of triangle $SAB$ at points $H, K$. On the segment $AB$, take points $M, N$ such that $\angle AHM = \angle BHI, \angle BKN = \angle AKI$.

b) On $BE, AF$, take the points $P, Q$ respectively so that $CP$ is parallel to $SE$ and $DQ$ is parallel to $SF$. The lines $SE, SF$ intersect the circumcircle of $SAB$, respectively, at $U, V$. Let $G$ be the intersection of $AU$ and $BV$. Prove that the median of vertex $G$ of the triangle $GPQ$ always passes through a fixed point.


## 2023 — 3

**Record:** `vnm_2023_29c942`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2023_29c942.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be an acute, non-isosceles triangle with circumcircle $(O)$. $BE$, $CF$ are the heights of $\triangle ABC$, and $BE$, $CF$ intersect at $H$. Let $M$ be the midpoint of $AH$, and $K$ be the point on $EF$ such that $HK$ is perpendicular to $EF$. A line not going through $A$ and parallel to $BC$ intersects the minor arcs $AB$ and $AC$ of $(O)$ at $P$, $Q$, respectively. Show that the tangent line of the circumcircle of $CQE$ at $E$, the tangent line of the circumcircle of $BPF$ at $F$, and $MK$ concur.


## 2024 — 3

**Record:** `ind_2024_1b53f9`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/india/ind_2024_1b53f9.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be an acute-angled triangle with $AB < AC$, and let $O, H$ be its circumcentre and orthocentre respectively. Points $Z, Y$ lie on segments $AB, AC$ respectively, such that
$$
\angle ZOB = \angle YOC = 90^\circ.
$$
The perpendicular line from $H$ to line $YZ$ meets lines $BO$ and $CO$ at $Q, R$ respectively. Let the tangents to the circumcircle of $\triangle AYZ$ at points $Y$ and $Z$ meet at point $T$. Prove that $Q, R, O, T$ are concyclic.

**Record:** `ind_2024_759b6c`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/india/ind_2024_759b6c.json.gz).

Let $\triangle ABC$ be an acute angled triangle with $AC > AB$ and incircle $\omega$. Let $\omega$ touch the sides $BC, CA$, and $AB$ at $D, E$, and $F$ respectively. Let $X$ and $Y$ be points outside $\triangle ABC$ satisfying
$$
\angle BDX = \angle XEA = \angle YDC = \angle AFY = 45^\circ.
$$
Prove that the circumcircles of $\triangle AXY$, $\triangle AEF$ and $\triangle ABC$ meet at a point $Z \neq A$.

**Record:** `ind_2024_804c67`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/india/ind_2024_804c67.json.gz).

A hunter picks three vertices of the polygon and he lays a trap which covers the interior and the boundary of the triangular region determined by them. Determine the minimum number of times he needs to do this to guarantee that the rabbit will be trapped.

**Record:** `ind_2024_8851c8`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/india/ind_2024_8851c8.json.gz).

## 2025 — Problem 4.

**Record:** `chn_2025_48f370`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/china/chn_2025_48f370.json.gz).

In convex quadrilateral $ABCD$, $AB \perp AD$ and $AD = DC$. Let point $E$ lie inside segment $BC$, and point $F$ lie on the extension of $DE$ such that $\angle ABF = \angle DEC > 90^\circ$. Let $O$ be the circumcenter of triangle $CDE$. Let $P$ be a point on the extension of $FO$ such that $FP = FB$. Let segment $BP$ intersect $AC$ at point $Q$.
Prove: $\angle AQB = \angle DPF$.


## 2025 — Problem 9.

**Record:** `chn_2025_843721`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/china/chn_2025_843721.json.gz).

Given a circle $\omega$ and two points $A, B$ outside $\omega$. A quadrilateral inscribed in $\omega$ is called "good" if one pair of opposite sides intersects at $A$ and the other pair intersects at $B$.
Assume that at least one good quadrilateral exists. Prove that there exists a good quadrilateral $\Gamma$ such that every other good quadrilateral has area smaller than $\Gamma$.


## 2025 — Problem 5.

**Record:** `chn_2025_f1f576`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/china/chn_2025_f1f576.json.gz).

Prove that there exist integers $x_1, x_2, \dots, x_{10}, y_1, y_2, \dots, y_{10}$ satisfying:
(1) For $i = 1, 2, \dots, 10$, $|x_i| \le 10^{10}$ and $|y_i| \le 10^{10}$;
(2) The point set in the plane
$$
X = \left\{ \left( \sum_{i=1}^{10} a_i x_i, \sum_{i=1}^{10} a_i y_i \right) \middle| a_1, a_2, \dots, a_{10} \in \{0, 1\} \right\}
$$
contains exactly 1024 distinct points;
(3) For any two parallel lines in the plane at distance 1, the strip region between them (including the lines) contains at most two points from $X$.


## 2025 — 4

**Record:** `usa_2025_08c332`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/united-states/usa_2025_08c332.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be a triangle, and let $X, Y$, and $Z$ be collinear points such that $AY = AZ$, $BZ = BX$, and $CX = CY$. Points $X'$, $Y'$, and $Z'$ are the reflections of $X$, $Y$, and $Z$ over $BC, CA$, and $AB$, respectively. Prove that if $X'Y'Z'$ is a nondegenerate triangle, then its circumcenter lies on the circumcircle of $ABC$.


## 2025 — 3

**Record:** `usa_2025_a27f25`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/united-states/usa_2025_a27f25.json.gz).

Let $A_1A_2\cdots A_{2025}$ be a convex 2025-gon, and let $A_i = A_{i+2025}$ for all integers $i$. Distinct points $P$ and $Q$ lie in its interior such that $\angle A_{i-1}A_iP = \angle QA_iA_{i+1}$ for all $i$. Define points $P_i^j$ and $Q_i^j$ for integers $i$ and positive integers $j$ as follows:
* For all $i$, $P_i^1 = Q_i^1 = A_i$.
* For all $i$ and $j$, $P_i^{j+1}$ and $Q_i^{j+1}$ are the circumcenters of $PP_i^jP_{i+1}^j$ and $QQ_i^jQ_{i+1}^j$, respectively.
Let $\mathcal{P}$ and $\mathcal{Q}$ be the polygons $P_1^{2025}P_2^{2025}\cdots P_{2025}^{2025}$ and $Q_1^{2025}Q_2^{2025}\cdots Q_{2025}^{2025}$, respectively.
(a) Prove that $\mathcal{P}$ and $\mathcal{Q}$ are cyclic.
(b) Let $O_P$ and $O_Q$ be the circumcenters of $\mathcal{P}$ and $\mathcal{Q}$, respectively. Assuming that $O_P \neq O_Q$, show that $O_P O_Q$ is parallel to $PQ$.


## 2025 — 6

**Record:** `usa_2025_abaaab`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/united-states/usa_2025_abaaab.json.gz).

Prove that there exists a real number $\varepsilon > 0$ such that there are infinitely many sequences of integers $0 < a_1 < a_2 < \dots < a_{2025}$ satisfying
$$
\text{gcd}(a_1^2 + 1, a_2^2 + 1, \dots, a_{2025}^2 + 1) > a_{2025}^{1+\varepsilon}.
$$


## 2025 — Problem 5

**Record:** `usa_2025_ed5827`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/united-states/usa_2025_ed5827.json.gz).

A tetrahedron $ABCD$ is said to be *angelic* if it has nonzero volume and satisfies
$$
\begin{gathered}
\angle BAC + \angle CAD + \angle DAB = \angle ABC + \angle CBD + \angle DBA, \\
\angle ACB + \angle BCD + \angle DCA = \angle ADB + \angle BDC + \angle CDA.
\end{gathered}
$$
Across all angelic tetrahedrons, what's the maximum number of distinct lengths that could appear in the set $\{AB, AC, AD, BC, BD, CD\}$?

**Record:** `usa_2025_fde337`. **Category:** selection. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/united-states/usa_2025_fde337.json.gz).

## 2006 — 11

**Record:** `jpn_2006_a56a34`. **Category:** pipeline supplement. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/japan/jpn_2006_a56a34.json.gz).

For $0 \le x, y \le 1$, let
$$
f(x, y) = x y^2 \sqrt{1-x^2} - x^2 y \sqrt{1-y^2}
$$

Find the minimum constant $c$ for which the following condition holds.
For any integer $n > 1$ and any real numbers $a_1, a_2, \dots, a_n$ such that $0 \le a_1 < a_2 < \dots < a_n \le 1$,
$$
f(a_1, a_2) + f(a_2, a_3) + \dots + f(a_{n-1}, a_n) < c
$$

$$
PQ = \frac{AB \cdot KQ}{BI}.
$$

## 2015 — Problem 6

**Record:** `rmm_2015_4db6ce`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romanian-master-of-mathematics-rmm/rmm_2015_4db6ce.json.gz).

Given a positive integer $n$, determine the largest real number $\mu$ satisfying the following condition: for every $4n$-point configuration $C$ in an open unit square $U$, there exists an open rectangle in $U$, whose sides are parallel to those of $U$, which contains exactly one point of $C$, and has an area greater than or equal to $\mu$.


## 2015 — Problem 6

**Record:** `rou_2015_eb480f`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romania/rou_2015_eb480f.json.gz).

Given a positive integer $n$, determine the largest real number $\mu$ satisfying the following condition: for every $4n$-point configuration $C$ in an open unit square $U$, there exists an open rectangle in $U$, whose sides are parallel to those of $U$, which contains exactly one point of $C$, and has an area greater than or equal to $\mu$.


## 2016 — Problem 5.

**Record:** `rou_2016_093c05`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romania/rou_2016_093c05.json.gz).

A convex hexagon $A_1B_1A_2B_2A_3B_3$ is inscribed in a circle $\Omega$ of radius $R$. The diagonals $A_1B_2$, $A_2B_3$, and $A_3B_1$ concur at $X$. For $i = 1, 2, 3$, let $\omega_i$ be the circle tangent to the segments $XA_i$ and $XB_i$, and to the arc $A_iB_i$ of $\Omega$ not containing other vertices of the hexagon; let $r_i$ be the radius of $\omega_i$.

a) Prove that $R \ge r_1 + r_2 + r_3$.

b) If $R = r_1 + r_2 + r_3$, prove that the six points where the circles $\omega_i$ touch the diagonals $A_1B_2$, $A_2B_3$, $A_3B_1$ are concyclic.


## 2016 — Problem 6.

**Record:** `rou_2016_185273`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romania/rou_2016_185273.json.gz).

A set of $n$ points in Euclidean 3-dimensional space, no four of which are coplanar, is partitioned into two subsets $A$ and $B$. An $\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}$-tree is a configuration of $n-1$ segments, each of which has an endpoint in $A$ and the other in $B$, and such that no segments form a closed polyline. An $\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}$-tree is transformed into another as follows: choose three distinct segments $A_1B_1$, $B_1A_2$ and $A_2B_2$ in the $\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}$-tree such that $A_1$ is in $A$ and $A_1B_1 + A_2B_2 > A_1B_2 + A_2B_1$, and remove the segment $A_1B_1$ to replace it by the segment $A_1B_2$. Given any $\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}$-tree, prove that every sequence of successive transformations comes to an end (no further transformation is possible) after finitely many steps.
Russia


## 2016 — Problem 1.

**Record:** `rou_2016_eef1e4`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romania/rou_2016_eef1e4.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be a triangle and let $D$ be a point on the segment $BC$, $D \neq B$ and $D \neq C$. The circle $ABD$ meets the segment $AC$ again at an interior point $E$. The circle $ACD$ meets the segment $AB$ again at an interior point $F$. Let $A'$ be the reflection of $A$ in the line $BC$. The lines $A'C$ and $DE$ meet at $P$, and the lines $A'B$ and $DF$ meet at $Q$.
Prove that the lines $AD$, $BP$ and $CQ$ are concurrent (or all parallel).


## 2017 — Problem 6

**Record:** `rou_2017_4320f5`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romania/rou_2017_4320f5.json.gz).

Let $ABCD$ be any convex quadrangle and let $P, Q, R, S$ be points on the segments $AB, BC, CD$, and $DA$, respectively. It is given that the segments $PR$ and $QS$ dissect $ABCD$ into four quadrangles, each of which has perpendicular diagonals. Show that the points $P, Q, R, S$ are concyclic.

Nikolai Beluhov, Bulgaria


## 2017 — Problem 4

**Record:** `rou_2017_6a40eb`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romania/rou_2017_6a40eb.json.gz).

In the Cartesian plane, let $\gamma_1$ and $\gamma_2$ be the graphs of the quadratic functions $f_1(x) = p_1x^2+q_1x+r_1$ and $f_2(x) = p_2x^2+q_2x+r_2$, where $p_1 > 0 > p_2$. The graphs $\gamma_1$ and $\gamma_2$ cross at distinct points $A$ and $B$. The four tangents to $\gamma_1$ and $\gamma_2$ at $A$ and $B$ form a convex quadrangle which has an inscribed circle. Prove that the graphs $\gamma_1$ and $\gamma_2$ have the same axis of symmetry.


## 2019 — Problem 4

**Record:** `rmm_2019_80fa3b`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romanian-master-of-mathematics-rmm/rmm_2019_80fa3b.json.gz).

Prove that for every positive integer $n$ there exists a (not necessarily convex) polygon with no three collinear vertices, which admits exactly $n$ different triangulations.
(A triangulation is a dissection of the polygon into triangles by interior diagonals which have no common interior points with each other nor with the sides of the polygon.)


## 2019 — Problem 2

**Record:** `rmm_2019_f4d767`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romanian-master-of-mathematics-rmm/rmm_2019_f4d767.json.gz).

Let $ABCD$ be an isosceles trapezoid with $AB \parallel CD$. Let $E$ be the midpoint of $AC$. Denote by $\omega$ and $\Omega$ the circumcircles of the triangles $ABE$ and $CDE$, respectively. Let $P$ be the crossing point of the tangent to $\omega$ at $A$ with the tangent to $\Omega$ at $D$. Prove that $PE$ is tangent to $\Omega$.


## 2023 — 2

**Record:** `rou_2023_9c5b23`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romania/rou_2023_9c5b23.json.gz).

Fix an integer $n \ge 3$. Let $S$ be a set of $n$ points in the plane, no three of which are collinear. Given pairwise distinct points $A, B, C$ in $S$, the triangle $ABC$ is suitable for $AB$ if area $ABC \le$ area $ABX$ for all $X$ in $S$ different from $A$ and $B$. (Note that for a segment $AB$ there could be several suitable triangles.) A triangle is adequate if its vertices are all in $S$ and it is suitable for at least two of its sides. Prove that there are at least $\frac{1}{2}(n-1)$ adequate triangles.
Bulgaria, Alexadar Ivanov


## 2024 — Problem 5.

**Record:** `rou_2024_05cf8c`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romania/rou_2024_05cf8c.json.gz).

Let $BC$ be a fixed segment in the plane, and let $A$ be a variable point in the plane not on the line $BC$. Distinct points $X$ and $Y$ are chosen on the rays $\vec{CA}$ and $\vec{BA}$, respectively, such that $\angle CBX = \angle YCB = \angle BAC$. Assume that the tangents to the circumcircle of $ABC$ at $B$ and $C$ meet line $XY$ at $P$ and $Q$, respectively, such that the points $X, P, Y$, and $Q$ are pairwise distinct and lie on the same side of $BC$. Let $\Omega_1$ be the circle through $X$ and $P$ centred on $BC$. Similarly, let $\Omega_2$ be the circle through $Y$ and $Q$ centred on $BC$. Prove that $\Omega_1$ and $\Omega_2$ intersect at two fixed points as $A$ varies.
Denmark, Daniel Pham Nguyen


## 2024 — Problem 3.

**Record:** `rou_2024_c3cc94`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/romania/rou_2024_c3cc94.json.gz).

Given a positive integer $n$, a set $S$ is *n-admissible* if:
• each element of $S$ is an unordered triple of distinct integers in $\{1, 2, \dots, n\}$,
• $|S| = n-2$, and
• for each $1 \le k \le n-2$ and each choice of $k$ distinct $A_1, A_2, \dots, A_k \in S$,
$$
|A_1 \cup A_2 \cup \dots \cup A_k| \ge k + 2.
$$

Is it true that, for all $n > 3$ and for each $n$-admissible set $S$, there exist pairwise distinct points $P_1, \dots, P_n$ in the plane such that the angles of the triangle $P_iP_jP_k$ are all less than $61^\circ$ for any triple $\{i, j, k\}$ in $S$?

Let $ABCD$ be a quadrilateral, and let $E$ and $F$ be points on sides $AD$ and $BC$, respectively, such that $AE/ED = BF/FC$. Ray $FE$ meets rays $BA$ and $CD$ at $S$ and $T$, respectively. Prove that the circumcircles of triangles $SAE$, $SBF$, $TCF$, and $TDE$ pass through a common point.


## 2008 — 4

**Record:** `usa_2008_2c2856`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/united-states/usa_2008_2c2856.json.gz).

Let $\mathcal{P}$ be a convex polygon with $n$ sides, $n \ge 3$. Any set of $n-3$ diagonals of $\mathcal{P}$ that do not intersect in the interior of the polygon determine a *triangulation* of $\mathcal{P}$ into $n-2$ triangles. If $\mathcal{P}$ is regular and there is a triangulation of $\mathcal{P}$ consisting of only isosceles triangles, find all the possible values of $n$.


## 2008 — 2

**Record:** `usa_2008_9389ce`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/united-states/usa_2008_9389ce.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be an acute, scalene triangle, and let $M$, $N$, and $P$ be the midpoints of $\overline{BC}$, $\overline{CA}$, and $\overline{AB}$, respectively. Let the perpendicular bisectors of $\overline{AB}$ and $\overline{AC}$ intersect ray $AM$ in points $D$ and $E$ respectively, and let lines $BD$ and $CE$ intersect in point $F$, inside of triangle $ABC$. Prove that points $A$, $N$, $F$, and $P$ all lie on one circle.

## 2010 — 1

Let $AXYZB$ be a convex pentagon inscribed in a semicircle of diameter $AB$. Denote by $P, Q, R, S$ the feet of the perpendiculars from $Y$ onto lines $AX$, $BX$, $AZ$, $BZ$, respectively. ## 2012 — 2

**Record:** `usa_2012_9b4f1a`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/united-states/usa_2012_9b4f1a.json.gz).

A circle is divided into $432$ congruent arcs by $432$ points. The points are colored in four colors such that some $108$ points are colored Red, some $108$ points are colored Green, some $108$ points are colored Blue, and the remaining $108$ points are colored Yellow. Prove that one can choose three points of each color in such a way that the four triangles formed by the chosen points of the same color are congruent.


## 2014 — 5.

**Record:** `usa_2014_59e29d`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/united-states/usa_2014_59e29d.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be a triangle with orthocenter $H$ and let $P$ be the second intersection of the circumcircle of triangle $AHC$ with the internal bisector of the angle $\angle BAC$. Let $X$ be the circumcenter of triangle $APB$ and $Y$ the orthocenter of triangle $APC$. Prove that the length of segment $XY$ is equal to the circumradius of triangle $ABC$.

(This problem was suggested by Titu Andreescu and Cosmin Pohoata.)


## 2014 — 3.

**Record:** `usa_2014_eef9b1`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/united-states/usa_2014_eef9b1.json.gz).

Prove that there exists an infinite set of points
$$
\dots, P_{-3}, P_{-2}, P_{-1}, P_0, P_1, P_2, P_3, \dots
$$
in the plane with the following property: For any three distinct integers $a, b$ and $c$, points $P_a, P_b$ and $P_c$ are collinear if and only if $a + b + c = 2014$.


## 2025 — USAMO 3

**Record:** `usa_2025_a5e142`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/united-states/usa_2025_a5e142.json.gz).

Alice the architect and Bob the builder play a game. First, Alice chooses two points $P$ and $Q$ in the plane and a subset $S$ of the plane, which are announced to Bob. Next, Bob marks infinitely many points in the plane, designating each a city. He may not place two cities within distance at most one unit of each other, and no three cities he places may be collinear. Finally, roads are constructed between the cities as follows: for each pair $A, B$ of cities, they are connected with a road along the line segment $AB$ if and only if the following condition holds:
For every city $C$ distinct from $A$ and $B$, there exists $R \in S$ such that $\triangle PQR$ is directly similar to either $\triangle ABC$ or $\triangle BAC$.

Alice wins the game if (i) the resulting roads allow for travel between any pair of cities via a finite sequence of roads and (ii) no two roads cross. Otherwise, Bob wins. Determine, with proof, which player has a winning strategy.


## 2025 — 4

**Record:** `usa_2025_f546c3`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/united-states/usa_2025_f546c3.json.gz).

Let $H$ be the orthocenter of acute triangle $ABC$, let $F$ be the foot of the altitude from $C$ to $AB$, and let $P$ be the reflection of $H$ across $BC$. Suppose that the circumcircle of triangle $AFP$ intersects line $BC$ at two distinct points $X$ and $Y$. Prove that $C$ is the midpoint of $XY$.

Let be given an acute triangle $ABC$ inscribed in a circle with center $O$ and two points $M$, $N$ on the line $AC$ such that $\overline{MN} = \overline{AC}$. Let $D$ be the orthogonal projection of $M$ on the line $BC$, $E$ be that of $N$ on the line $AB$.

## 2003 — 2

**Record:** `vnm_2003_491dad`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2003_491dad.json.gz).

In plane, let be given two fixed circles $(O_1)$ and $(O_2)$ touching each other at $M$, the radius of $(O_2)$ is greater than that of $(O_1)$. $A$ is the point on $(O_2)$ such that the points $O_1$, $O_2$, $A$ are not collinear. Let $AB$ and $AC$ be the tangents to $(O_1)$ with touching points $B$ and $C$. The lines $MB$ and $MC$ cut again $(O_2)$ respectively at $E$ and $F$. Let $D$ be the point of intersection of the line $EF$ and tangent to $(O_2)$ at $A$. Prove that $D$ moves on a fixed line when $A$ moves on $(O_2)$ so that the three points $O_1$, $O_2$, $A$ are not collinear.


## 2005 — Problem 3.

**Record:** `vnm_2005_05160d`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2005_05160d.json.gz).

In plane, let be given an octagon $A_1A_2A_3A_4A_5A_6A_7A_8$ such that no three of its diagonals are concurrent. Each point of intersection of every two diagonals of the octagon is called a cross.
Consider the convex quadrilaterals whose vertices are vertices of the given octagon. Each such quadrilateral is called a subquadrilateral.
Find the least positive integer $n$ such that one can colour $n$ crosses so that for every $i, k \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8\}$ and $i \ne k$, if we denote by $s(i, k)$ the number of

subquadrilaterals admitting $A_i, A_k$ as vertices and admitting a coloured cross as the point of intersection of the diagonals then the values $s(i, k)$ are all equal.


## 2005 — Problem 2.

**Record:** `vnm_2005_7f10fc`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2005_7f10fc.json.gz).

In plane, let be given a fixed circle $(O)$ with radius $R$, two fixed points $A$, $B$ on the circle $(O)$ such that $A$, $B$, $O$ are not collinear.
Let $C$ be a point on $(O)$ distinct from $A$ and $B$. Construct the circle $(O_1)$ passing through $A$, touching the line $BC$ at $C$, construct the circle $(O_2)$ passing through $B$, touching the line $AC$ at $C$. The circle $(O_1)$ and $(O_2)$ intersect again at $D$ distinct from $C$.
Prove that
$$
i) \, CD \le R;$$
ii) The line $CD$ passes through a fixed point when $C$ moves on $(O)$ so that $C$ does not coincide with $A$ and $B$. 
($(O)$ denotes a circle with center $O$.)

**Record:** `vnm_2011_accee2`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2011_accee2.json.gz).

Given a convex pentagon $ABCDE$, of which the length of each edge and of the diagonals $AC$, $AD$ does not exceed $\sqrt{3}$. Choose 2001 arbitrary distinct points in the interior of that pentagon. Show that there exists a unit disk with center lying on the edges of the pentagon, which contains at least 403 of the chosen points.


## 2012 — Problem 2.

**Record:** `vnm_2012_2a1811`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2012_2a1811.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be a triangle inscribed in a circle $(O)$. Let $D$ be a point on the arc $BC$ not containing $A$. Suppose that the tangent of $(O)$ at $D$ meets $BC$ at $E$. The circumcircle of $ODE$ meets $AD$ at second point $F$ (different to $D$). The line $EF$ meets $AB$ at $M$, the line $DM$ meet $(O)$ at the second point $K$ (different to $D$).
Prove that $K,F,C$ are collinear.


## 2012 — Problem 3.

**Record:** `vnm_2012_65cc7c`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2012_65cc7c.json.gz).

Let $\triangle ABC$ be a triangle inscribed in a circle $(O)$. The point $P$ is an arbitrary point on interior angle bisector $AD$ of triangle $ABC$, $D$ belongs to segment $BC$ and $P$ lies between $A$ and $D$. The line $BP$ meets segment $AC$ at $E$, the line $CP$ meets segment $AB$ at $F$. The projection of $P$ on segment $BC$ is $M$. Suppose that two lines $PM, EF$ intersect at $N$ and $AN$ meets $OP$ at $L$. Prove that the line $ML$ always passes through a certain fixed point when $P$ is moving on $AD$.


## 2012 — Problem 3.

**Record:** `vnm_2012_8a629d`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2012_8a629d.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be the isosceles triangle with $AB = AC$ and $M,N$ are the midpoints of $AB, AC$, respectively. The circle $(K)$ is tangent to line $AB$ at $B$ and tangent to $AC$ at $C$. An arbitrary circle $(I)$ tangents to $(K)$, passes through $A$ and cuts the line $MN$ at $D,E$. Prove that:

a. Point $I$ belongs to a fixed line and the angle $\angle MAN$ is a constant.

b. The circle with diameter $DE$ is always tangent to two certain fixed circles and has the center belongs to the line $AK$.


## 2012 — Problem 5.

**Record:** `vnm_2012_99501a`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2012_99501a.json.gz).

Let $a, b, c$ be positive real numbers. It is known that the system of equations
$$
\begin{cases} a^2x + b^2y + c^2z = 1 \\ xy + yz + zx = 1 \end{cases} \quad \text{(where $a, b, c$ are parameters)}
$$
has only one solution $(x, y, z)$. Prove that $a, b, c$ are the sides of a certain triangle.


## 2012 — 3

**Record:** `vnm_2012_e2d8cc`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2012_e2d8cc.json.gz).

Let $ABCD$ be a convex quadrilateral inscribed in a circle $(O)$. Suppose that the line $AB$ meets the line $CD$ at $M$ and the line $AD$ meets the line $BC$ at $N$. Let $P, Q, S$, and $T$ be the intersection points of the bisectors of the angles $\widehat{MAN}$ and $\widehat{MBN}$, $\widehat{MBN}$ and $\widehat{MCN}$, $\widehat{MCN}$ and $\widehat{MDN}$, $\widehat{MDN}$ and $\widehat{MAN}$, respectively. Suppose that $P, Q, S$ and $T$ are pairwise different.

1. Prove that four points $P, Q, S$ and $T$ are cyclic. Let $I$ be the center of the cycle $(P, Q, S, T)$.

2. Let $E$ be the intersection of $AC$ and $BD$. Prove that three points $E, O, I$ are collinear.


## 2013 — Problem 6.

**Record:** `vnm_2013_c284da`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2013_c284da.json.gz).

Given a $10 \times 10 \times 10$ box of $1000$ unit white cubes. An and Binh play a game with this box. An selects some bands of size $1 \times 1 \times 10$ such that any two chosen bands have no common points and then changes all cells on these bands to black. Binh then selects some unit cubes and asks An what color of these cells. What is the least number of cells Binh must choose in order to determine all black cells based on An's answer?


## 2013 — Problem 4.

**Record:** `vnm_2013_f344b9`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2013_f344b9.json.gz).

The diagonals of a convex pentagon cut together divide the pentagon into one small pentagon and 10 sub-triangles. Find the maximum number of triangles among these sub-triangles such that they have the same area.


## 2014 — 4

**Record:** `vnm_2014_80fcea`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2014_80fcea.json.gz).

Given an acute, scalene triangle $ABC$ ($AB < AC$) with the circumcircle $(O)$. Let $I$ be the midpoint of arc $BC$ that does not contain $A$. Take $K$ on $AC$ ($K \neq C$) such that $IK = IC$. Line $BK$ intersects $(O)$ again at $D$ and intersects $AI$ at $E$. $DI$ meets $AC$ at $F$.

a) Prove that $EF = \frac{BC}{2}$.

b) Let $M$ be the point on $DI$ such that $CM$ is parallel to $AD$. Line $KM$ meets $BC$ at $N$ and the circumcircle of triangle $BKN$ meets $(O)$ again at $P$. Prove that $PK$ passes through the midpoint of the segment $AD$.


## 2015 — 4

**Record:** `vnm_2015_be292d`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2015_be292d.json.gz).

Given a circumcircle $O$ and two fixed points $B, C$ on $O$ ($BC$ is not the diameter of $O$). A point $A$ is moving on $O$ such that $ABC$ is an acute triangle. Let $E, F$ be the feet of the altitudes from $B, C$ of triangle $ABC$. Let $I$ be an arbitrary circle passing through $E$ and $F$.

a) Let $I$ touch $BC$ at $D$. Prove that $\frac{DB}{DC} = \sqrt{\frac{\cot B}{\cot C}}$.

b) Suppose that $I$ intersects $BC$ at $M$ and $N$. Let $H$ be the orthocenter of triangle $ABC$ and $P, Q$ be the intersections of $I$ and the circumcircle of triangle $HBC$. Let $K$ be the circle passing through $P, Q$ and touches $O$ at $T$ ($T$ is on the same side with $A$ with respect to $PQ$). Prove that the interior angle bisector of $\angle MTN$ passes through a fixed point.


## 2016 — 3

**Record:** `vnm_2016_638715`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2016_638715.json.gz).

Given a triangle $ABC$ with fixed vertices $B$, $C$; point $A$ moves such that triangle $ABC$ is acute. Let $D$ be the midpoint of $BC$ and $E$, $F$ be the projections of $D$ to $AB$, $AC$ respectively.

a) Let $O$ be the circumcenter of triangle $ABC$. $EF$ meets $AO$ and $BC$ at $M$, $N$ respectively. Prove that the circumcircle of triangle $AMN$ passes through a fixed point.

b) Suppose that the tangents of the circumcircle of triangle $AEF$ at $E$, $F$ intersect each other at $T$. Prove that $T$ lies on a fixed line.


## 2016 — 6

**Record:** `vnm_2016_787e4a`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2016_787e4a.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be an acute, scalene triangle with the circumcircle ($O$) and angles $ACB$, $ABC$ are acute. Let $M$ be a point on arc $BC$ that does not contain $A$ and $AM$ is not perpendicular to $BC$. The line $AM$ meets the perpendicular bisector of $BC$ at $T$ and the circumcircle of triangle $AOT$ meets $O$ at $N$ ($N \neq A$).

a) Prove that $\angle BAM = \angle CAN$.

b) Let $I$ be the incenter of triangle $ABC$ and $G$ be the foot of the internal angle bisector of $\angle BAC$. Let $AI$, $MI$ and $NI$ intersect ($O$) at $D$, $E$ and $F$ respectively. Let $P$, $Q$ be the intersections of $DF$ and $AM$, $DE$ and $AN$. The circle passing through $P$ and touches $AD$ at $I$ meets $DF$ at $H$ ($H \neq D$). Similarly, the circle passing through $Q$ and touches $AD$ at $I$ meets $DE$ at $K$ ($K \neq D$). Prove that the circumcircle of triangle $GHK$ touches $BC$.


## 2018 — 4

**Record:** `vnm_2018_949f1a`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2018_949f1a.json.gz).

On the Cartesian plane, let $C$ be the graph of the function $y = \sqrt[3]{x^2}$. A line $d$ varies on the plane such that $d$ always cuts $C$ at three distinct points with $x$-coordinates $x_1, x_2$ and $x_3$.

a. Prove that the following value is a constant:
$$
\sqrt[3]{\frac{x_1 x_2}{x_3^2}} + \sqrt[3]{\frac{x_2 x_3}{x_1^2}} + \sqrt[3]{\frac{x_3 x_1}{x_2^2}}
$$

b. Show that:
$$
\sqrt[3]{\frac{x_1^2}{x_2 x_3}} + \sqrt[3]{\frac{x_2^2}{x_3 x_1}} + \sqrt[3]{\frac{x_3^2}{x_1 x_2}} < -\frac{15}{4}
$$


## 2018 — 3

**Record:** `vnm_2018_b1f4be`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2018_b1f4be.json.gz).

An investor has two rectangular lands, each of size $120 \times 100$.

a. On the first land, she wants to build a house with a rectangular base of size $25 \times 35$ and nine circular flower pots with diameter $5$ outside the house. Prove that for all positions of the flower pots, the remaining land is still sufficient to build the desired house.

b. On the second land, she wants to construct a polygonal fish pond such that the distance from an arbitrary point on the land, outside the pond, to the nearest pond edge is not over $5$. Prove that the perimeter of the pond is not smaller than $440 - 20\sqrt{2}$.


## 2018 — Problem 3

**Record:** `vnm_2018_da4108`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2018_da4108.json.gz).

A farmer has 2 rectangle lands of size $120 \text{ m} \times 100 \text{ m}$.

a. On the first land, there are 9 circle gardens of diameter $5 \text{ m}$. Prove that regardless to the position of gardens, he always can builds a rectangle garden of size $25 \text{ m} \times 35 \text{ m}$.

b. On the second land, he builds a convex polygon lake such that the shortest distance from any point on the boundary of the land to the lake is at most $5$ m. Prove that the perimeter of the lake is at least $440 - 20\sqrt{2}$ m.


## 2021 — 3

**Record:** `vnm_2021_25d37d`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2021_25d37d.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be an acute, scalene triangle with orthocenter $H$ and $D$, $E$, $F$ be the feet of altitudes from vertices $A$, $B$, $C$ respectively. Let $(I)$ be the circumcircle of triangle $HEF$ with centre $I$ and $K$, $J$ be the midpoints of $BC$, $EF$ respectively. $HJ$ meets $(I)$ again at $G$, $GK$ meets $(I)$ again at $L$.

a) Prove that $AL$ is perpendicular to $EF$.

b) Let $AL$ meet $EF$ at $M$, $IM$ meet the circumcircle of triangle $IEF$ again at $N$ and $DN$ meet $AB$, $AC$ at $P$, $Q$ respectively. Prove that $PE$, $QF$ and $AK$ are concurrent.


## 2021 — 7

**Record:** `vnm_2021_7c1c6f`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2021_7c1c6f.json.gz).

Let $D$ be the intersection of the two tangent lines of $(O)$ at $B$ and $C$. The circle passing through $A$ and tangent to $BC$ at $B$ intersects the median passing $A$ of the triangle $ABC$ at $G$. Lines $BG$, $CG$ intersect $CD$, $BD$ at $E, F$ respectively.

a) The line passing through the midpoints of $BE$ and $CF$ cuts $BF$, $CE$ at $M, N$ respectively. Prove that the points $A, D, M$ and $N$ lie on a same circle.

b) Lines $AD, AG$ intersect the circumcircle of $DBC, GBC$ at $H, K$ respectively. The perpendicular bisectors of $HK, HE$, and $HF$ cut $BC, CA$ and $AB$ at $R, P$ and $Q$ respectively. Prove that the points $R, P$ and $Q$ are collinear.


## 2022 — 7

**Record:** `vnm_2022_03d689`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2022_03d689.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be an acute triangle with fixed points $B, C$ and point $A$ moves on the big arc $BC$ of $(ABC)$ such that $AB \neq AC$. Incircle $(I)$ of triangle $ABC$ touches $BC$ at $D$. Let $I_a$ be the $A$-excenter of triangle $ABC$. $I_aD$ cuts $OI$ at $L$ and $E$ lies on $(I)$ such that $DE$ is parallel to $AI$.

a) $LE$ cuts $AI$ at $F$. Prove that $AF = AI$.

b) Let $M$ be the point the circumcircle $(J)$ of triangle $I_aBC$ such that $I_aM$ is parallel to $AI$. The line $MD$ cuts $(J)$ again at $N$. Prove that the midpoint $T$ of $MN$ lies on a fixed circle.


## 2022 — Problem 3

**Record:** `vnm_2022_3ef87d`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2022_3ef87d.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be a triangle. Points $E, F$ move on the opposite ray of $BA, CA$ such that $BF = CE$. Let $M, N$ be the midpoints of $BE, CF$. Suppose that $BF$ cuts $CE$ at $D$.

a) Let $I, J$ be the centers of circumcircles of triangles $DBE$ and $DCF$. Prove that $MN$ is parallel to $IJ$.

b) Let $K$ be the midpoint of $MN$ and $H$ be the orthocenter of triangle $AEF$. Prove that when $E$ moves on the opposite ray of $BA$, line $HK$ goes through a fixed point.


## 2023 — 7

**Record:** `vnm_2023_938036`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2023_938036.json.gz).

Let $ABC$ be a scalene triangle with orthocenter $H$ and circumcenter $O$. Incircle $(I)$ of the $ABC$ is tangent to the sides $BC, CA, AB$ at $M, N, P$ respectively. Denote $\Omega_A$ to be the circle passing through point $A$, external tangent to $(I)$ at $A'$ and cut again $AB, AC$ at $A_b, A_c$ respectively. The circles $\Omega_B, \Omega_C$ and points $B', B_a, B_c, C', C_a, C_b$ are defined similarly.

a) Prove that $B_cC_b + C_aA_c + A_bB_a \ge NP + PM + MN$.

b) Suppose $A', B', C'$ lie on $AM, BN, CP$ respectively. Denote $K$ as the circumcenter of the triangle formed by lines $A_bA_c, B_cB_a, C_aC_b$. Prove $OH$ is parallel to $IK$.


## 2024 — 7

**Record:** `vnm_2024_9e7ac6`. **Category:** contest. [Source](https://mathnet.mit.edu/data_v2/full/vietnam/vnm_2024_9e7ac6.json.gz).

In the space, there is a convex polyhedron $D$ such that for every vertex of $D$, there are an even number of edges passing through that vertex. We choose a face $F$ of $D$. Then we assign each edge of $D$ a positive integer such that for all faces of $D$ different from $F$, the sum of the numbers assigned on the edges of that face is a positive integer divisible by 2024. Prove that the sum of the numbers assigned on the edges of $F$ is also a positive integer divisible by 2024.
